## Goal

Everything added after the first report set shipped: the miss and rework economics page, the phase-effort page, the Playbook axis of every report, imported telemetry for private and corporate repositories, and the 2026-09-07 stream fields that say whose gap a miss was. The largest phase, and the one still being built.

**Phase 3 — Depth — misses, phase effort, the Playbook axis, imported sources.** The other phases are `docs/TfLens-Checklist.md`, `docs/TfLens-P2-Checklist.md`; `docs/TfLens-Phases.md` is the map.

| REQ-FN-134 | System shall publish **`duration_recomputed_n`**, the number of records whose timestamps **overrode a stored `duration_s` that disagreed with them by more than one second**. The tolerance is one second so that ordinary rounding is never reported as a disagreement. | REQ-FN-135 | *(amended 2026-09-10 — see the amendment below; the rule below is the one in force)* System shall take a run's `duration_s` **from the record's own `started` and `ended` whenever both of them parse**, shall **override a stored `duration_s` that disagrees with those timestamps by more than one second**, shall treat a record whose **`ended` precedes its `started` as carrying no duration at all** and exclude it from every duration figure, shall count a run that **starts and ends in the same second as recording no elapsed time** rather than as corrupt, and shall fall back to a **positive stored `duration_s` only where the timestamps cannot be read**, treating `ts` as `ended` where `ended` is absent (SCHEMA's own definition). It shall state **how many durations were derived** beside every time figure built on them. A summed `duration_s` that skips records carrying two clocks and no duration **counts them as zero time while still counting their tokens** — so a phase's time covers one set of runs and its tokens another. The framework's reset reported **16h49m** under that bug against a true **55h57m**. The derivation is a **read-time** operation: TfLens writes to no stream (§3) and nothing is backfilled | N/A | 0 | 2026-09-11 verify: N/A — duplicate of REQ-FN-112, which already covers BRD-179. | REQ-FN-140 | System shall honour **`kind: "run-void"`** (SCHEMA §2.7): a correction record naming one run by `cmd` + `started` and saying why. The named run shall leave **every** figure — never clamped, halved or guessed at — the void record shall never itself be counted as a run, and the counts shall be published as **`runs_voided_n`**, the reasons as `runs_voided`, and a void naming no run on the stream as **`run_voids_orphaned_n`**. Neither record shall ever be edited or deleted. Until this shipped TfLens read **81 live runs on TechieFlow against the reference's 75** — three corrections counted as work, and the three wrong runs they corrected still inside every total | Verified | 100% | 2026-09-13 verify: not verified — no test named REQ-FN-140 ran | [view](#d-req-fn-140) |
| REQ-FN-141 | System shall identify a run record by its **line in its source file**, not by `(ts, app, cmd)`. That key is not unique: two `log-miss` runs land in the same second, and so do two `run-void` records naming different runs — and collapsing the latter silently un-voids a run. The streams are append-only, so line *n* is always the same record: re-parsing a file shall remain the no-op BRD-28 requires, while **two byte-identical records shall both survive** | Verified | 100% | 2026-09-13 verify: not verified — no test named REQ-FN-141 ran | [view](#d-req-fn-141) |
| REQ-UI-072 | User can see, on a **Price providers** screen, every provider a rate comes from — starting with Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenCode Go and OpenRouter — with each model's published rate, the page a person can check it against, and **when it was last checked**. User can add a provider and its endpoint, add or change a rate, and remove a provider. A provider that publishes rates as data shall be **refreshed from its own endpoint**; one that does not shall be marked as typed from its published page, and the two shall be **visibly different claims**. A rate the endpoint declines to publish (OpenRouter's `-1`) shall be **skipped, never stored and never coerced to zero**. A refresh that fails shall leave the stored rates unchanged and say so. Where two providers price the same model the first shall win and the clash shall be **reported, never averaged** — the mean of two published rates is a number nobody publishes | Needs re-verify | 75% | 2026-09-13 verify: ⚠ mockup-parity — missing on theme-toggle > span[0] @1280: the mockup draws a badge/pill here ("") and the app renders no such element — th (tests/.artifacts/verify/parity.json) | [view](#d-req-ui-072) |
| REQ-FN-142 | A superseded model shall **keep its rate**. A record naming an older model is history and still has to report what it cost; dropping the rate would move those runs into `list_usd_unpriced_n` and make a past phase read as cheaper than it was | Verified | 100% | 2026-09-13 verify: not verified — no test named REQ-FN-142 ran | [view](#d-req-fn-142) |

**Status values:** `Not Started` · `In Progress` · `Implemented` (code done, not yet verified) · `Verified` (self-smoke or verifier PASS — acceptance AND data-render AND visual gates all pass) · `Done (pre-existing)` (migrated from an earlier dev plan as already complete — build agents must NOT rebuild; terminal like `Verified`) · `Needs re-verify` (a defect or change was logged — must be re-run before it can return to `Verified`) · `PARTIAL` (some acceptance unmet — say what in Remarks) · `FAIL` (verifier ran and failed — bug in Remarks) · `Blocked` (external/library gap — link the TR-/TR-RAG- entry in Remarks) · `N/A`.
**% guide:** `0` not started · `25` scaffolded · `50` in progress · `75` implemented-unverified · `100` verified.
**Remarks:** date + what was done / what is missing / bug or library reference. This is the home for bugs and change notes — do not spawn a separate file. Visual-gate failures are prefixed `⚠ visual:`; security findings `⚠ SECURITY`.

  - *History:* 2026-09-09 [REQ-UI-039] `Components/Pages/Coverage.razor` + `TfLens.Core/Contracts/CoverageDataQuality.cs` + `TfLens.Core/Metrics/DataQualityFigures.cs`. The 2026-09-08 amendment's **Data quality** band is built control-for-control against the refreshed `docs/mockups/coverage.html`: a plain `h3` heading with the standing copy *"None of these is a warning and none appears on the Misses KPI row"* (`data-quality`), then four cards. **`cov-field-completeness`** — Repo · Misses · No `sort` · No `what` · Eligible, one row per repository and an `All repos` total, with the footnote stating the predating count **in those words**; on the smoke dataset it reads *172 of 172 misses carry no `sort`, and 172 of them predate the field*, which is BRD-127's own sentence arriving as a measurement rather than as prose. The two columns are deliberately **not** pooled: `Eligible` comes from `LateGateCoverageCalculator.IsEligibleForField` against `MetricsConstants.FieldSince`, so the 2026-09-07 floor is read from the one table and the one code path (BRD-172). **`cov-derived`** — Field · Derived · How for `duration_s` (`ended − started`, `ts` standing in for an absent `ended`) and `attempt`, with *"Nothing is written back"* on its face; the counts are what REQ-FN-112 / REQ-FN-113's derivations will have to answer for, and this card is the "state the derived count beside the figure" half of BRD-179/BRD-180. **`cov-unrecognised-values`** — Where · Value · Records over the `runs.cmd`, `gates.verdict` and `harness` vocabularies SCHEMA.md §1–§3 close; the live smoke found **76 records** (`PASS` 66, `In Progress` 4, `NOT-OBSERVABLE` 4, `rename-page` 2) that a reader filtering to a known list would have lost silently (BRD-181). **`cov-miss-diagnostics`** keeps the original BRD-127 facts and gains three: amendments folded at read time (BRD-176), `review` records read — *stated because they are **not** misses* (BRD-174) — and the framework's own `req_class: FR` verdicts kept separate (BRD-177). Misses are read **folded** (`MissAmendFolder.Fold`) before any field is counted, so a `sort` supplied by a later `miss-amend` reads as carried rather than as missing. The old `miss-quality` card is not deleted but becomes the band's wrapper id, so it is still absent on the Playbook axis — a stream that does not exist reports nothing, not zero. Nothing was written to the parser, the records or the storage: `Sort` / `What` / `MissReviewRecord` / `ReadMissReviewsAsync` all landed with the concurrent FN cluster and are **bound**, not re-implemented. Self-smoke (`.tfcore/utils/tf-verify-boot.sh` on :5117, userId 2 `tflensdemo@techierathore.com`, headless Playwright, dataset seeded through the app's own **Import metric files** journey with this repository's `docs/metrics/` — 1,075 records, 0 invalid lines): **7 passed / 2 skipped**, `VISUAL @1280 clean`, `VISUAL @390 clean`, render gate **coverage 44 controls, 0 RENDER-EMPTY / 0 RENDER-ERROR**, mockup-parity **23 elements compared, 1 finding (1 waived — the pre-existing cross-screen `app-sidebar` clip), 0 new**. .NET **701/701** Core + **120/120** Guardrails, `tf-build.sh` PASS 0 warnings. ⚠ carried, not built: the `/misses` surfaces for the same fields (REQ-UI-052..REQ-UI-054) and the derivations themselves (REQ-FN-112, REQ-FN-113) belong to their own rows; Coverage states the counts, it does not derive the values.


### Phase 3 — whose gap it was (added 2026-09-08, F-MISS)

*Source: `docs/TfLens-Metrics-Update-Prompt.md`, checked against `.tfcore/telemetry/SCHEMA.md`. The fields exist in every project's streams already — nothing upstream has to change, only the reader.*

<a id="d-req-ui-052"></a>
- **REQ-UI-052** — `/misses` gains a **"Whose gap was it"** band: the `sort` distribution rendered **in words** — *"there was a check and it did not catch it"*, never `weak-check` — sitting beside the existing miss-class distribution without merging into it, with a filter, and with the fixed response for each bucket named on the row (BRD-171, BRD-172). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/misses.html.
  - *Acceptance:* When a user reads the whose-gap band on `/misses`, then each answer is in words with `n of N sorted` on the card and the predating count stated apart.
  - *Checks:* When the owner opens `/misses`, then the whose-gap band shows each answer in words with `n of N sorted` on its face and the predates-the-field count stated separately.
  - *History:* 2026-09-09 [REQ-UI-052] `Components/Pages/Misses.razor` + `Misses.razor.css`. The band is `miss-sort`, a `Card` sitting **after** the origin × class / failed-practice pair and never inside either — the two answer different questions and the smoke asserts neither card contains the other. Nothing here computes a figure: the counts and shares come straight off `MissSegmentFigures.SortDistribution` / `SortN` / `SortEligibility` / `SortUnrecognised` / `AmendedValues`, which the concurrent FN cluster landed; this row added **the English**, one table in one place (`SortVocabulary`), read by the band, by the per-miss column and by the filter, so `weak-check` never reaches a bucket label. All four answers get a row whether or not they were observed — the same shape the failed-practice card already uses for its seven — and a stored value **outside** the four keeps its own row under its own name, marked *"Not one of the four — stored as it stands, counted, and never coerced"* (BRD-170). `miss-sort-denominator` carries `n of N sorted` on the card face where **N is `SortEligibility.Eligible`**, never the miss count, so no `sort` figure is ever a percentage of all misses (BRD-172). The footer states the predating count **apart**, in those words, beside the unanswered count and the unrecognised count — three separate figures that are never pooled — plus `miss-sort-amended` for the answers a `miss-amend` completed at read time (BRD-176). The filter BRD-171 asks for is the per-miss table's own toolbar search over a new **Whose gap** column carrying the words: the smoke types a whose-gap phrase and asserts the grid narrows, which is the only proof that the search actually reaches the column. Self-smoke on a build in its own output directory (`:5142`, userId 2 `tflensdemo@techierathore.com`, headless Playwright): the live estate read **6 of 7 sorted · 91 misses predate the field (2026-09-07) · 1 answer arrived by amend · 1 value outside the four** (`weak-checks`, rendered as its own row and not folded into `weak-check`) — the unrecognised-value path exercised by real data rather than by a fixture. `tests/verify/ui-misses-whose-gap.spec.ts` **7/7**, `tf-build.sh` PASS 0 warnings, no horizontal overflow at 1280 or 390. ⚠ carried, not built: the engine-side `sort` figures, the parser and the storage are the FN cluster's rows — this row binds them and adds no rule of its own.
<a id="d-req-ui-053"></a>
- **REQ-UI-053** — Every row of the per-miss table carries the record's own one-sentence `what` description in the owner's words, and that sentence is the only prose on the page that came from a stream (BRD-173, BRD-84). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/misses.html.
  - *Acceptance:* When a user reads a miss row on `/misses`, then it carries the record's own one-sentence description, or says the record predates the field.
  - *Checks:* When the owner opens `/misses`, then every row shows the record's own one-sentence description, and a record predating the field says so rather than showing a blank.
  - *History:* 2026-09-09 [REQ-UI-053] `Components/Pages/Misses.razor` + `Misses.razor.css`. The first detail column becomes **Miss — what happened**, exactly as `docs/mockups/misses.html` draws it: the miss id, and under it the record's own `what` sentence at `data-testid="miss-what"`, rendered verbatim — nothing truncated, reworded or joined to anything else. A record carrying none says which absence it is rather than showing a blank: *"No description — this record predates the field (7 September 2026)"* when it is outside the `what` floor, *"No description recorded on this miss"* when it is inside it, the eligibility read from the engine's own `LateGateCoverageCalculator.IsEligibleForField` against `MetricsConstants.FieldSince` rather than from a date written here. `miss-what-note` states the rule the sentence lives under — it is the **only prose that travels** from a stream onto this page, and requirement text is never stored, rendered or exported (BRD-84 unrelaxed) — and names how many rows carry one and how many predate the field. `sort` and `what` were also added to the raw-record disclosure, which is where the folded-versus-stored distinction is already explained. `.tflens-miss-cell` gives the cell a floor and a ceiling so a long sentence neither squeezes the columns beside it nor stretches the table past the card it scrolls inside. Self-smoke (`:5142`, userId 2, headless Playwright): the spec walks **every rendered row** and fails on any blank `what`; on the live estate all 89 rows carried the predates-the-field sentence, and on the imported probe they carried the owners' own sentences. `tests/verify/ui-misses-whose-gap.spec.ts` **7/7**, `tf-build.sh` PASS 0 warnings.
<a id="d-req-ui-054"></a>
- **REQ-UI-054** — `/misses` gains a **"What reviews cost"** band: one row per review phase carrying corrections given, cost to produce the reviewed output and cost to apply the corrections (BRD-175). *Mockup:* docs/mockups/misses.html.
  - *Acceptance:* When a user reads the review band on `/misses`, then corrections and both costs show per phase, and an absent cost reads not available.
  - *Checks:* When the owner opens `/misses`, then the review band shows corrections and both costs per phase, and an absent cost reads `not available` rather than zero.
  - *History:* 2026-09-09 [REQ-UI-054] `Components/Pages/Misses.razor`. A fifth band — heading, the standing alert *"The only record anywhere that prices a specification defect"*, and `miss-review-cost` carrying Review phase · Corrections · Cost to produce · Cost to correct · Correction as share, with `copied · never computed` on its face. The page reads `ITelemetryStore.ReadMissReviewsAsync` into a list of its own: reviews are **never** handed to `MissFigures.Compute`, never folded and never counted, and the smoke asserts no review phase appears in the per-miss table, the origin cross-tab or the whose-gap distribution (BRD-174). Every figure is the nullable the record carries, so an absent one renders **`not available`** and never `0` — the case BRD-175 was written for, and the live estate supplies it: two of the three stored records name their produce run but carry no produce window. *Correction as share* is the ratio of the two copied figures and refuses to appear unless both are present; it is stated on the card as a ratio and not a third cost, so nothing here computes, re-derives or apportions a review cost. Rows are **one per record**, ordered by the phase vocabulary, rather than a total per phase: adding two copied token windows would produce a figure nobody measured. A phase with **no** record gets no row at all — an invented row of `not available`s reads as a review that happened and cost nothing — and the absence is named in the footer instead (the smoke's estate has no `handoff-review`). Self-smoke (`:5142`, userId 2, headless Playwright, data seeded through the app's own **Import metric files** journey with this repository's `docs/metrics/`, and the probe removed again so no other cluster's figures moved): three rows, `day1-review` 3 corrections · produce `not available` · correct 178,645. `tests/verify/ui-misses-whose-gap.spec.ts` **7/7**, `tf-build.sh` PASS 0 warnings, no horizontal overflow at 1280 or 390. **Also amended:** `tests/verify/cluster-a-misses-review.spec.ts` asserted `day1-review` appeared **nowhere** on `/misses` — correct while TfLens had no review surface, and directly contradicted by this row. It now asserts the narrower and stronger property BRD-174 actually protects: no review phase reaches the per-miss table, the origin cross-tab or the whose-gap band, and the only band that may name one is the review band. Re-run **5/5**.

<a id="d-req-fn-072"></a>
- **REQ-FN-072** — `AddRecord` dispatches on each record's own `kind` **within** `StreamKind.Misses`: `miss` → `MissRecord`, `miss-fix` → `MissFixRecord`, `miss-amend` → `MissAmendRecord`; anything else increments `InvalidLines` and is skipped (BRD-113).
  - *Acceptance:* When a miss stream is parsed from `/repos`, then each record dispatches on its own kind and an unknown kind is counted and skipped, never thrown.
  - *Checks:* all three kinds parse from **one** file in a single pass; an unknown `kind` is counted, not thrown (same contract as REQ-FN-032) and never fails a sync; absent optionals stay `null` and are never coerced to `0` or to a bucket (the `StoreNullVsZero` discipline, on every nullable of all three records); unknown properties reach `Overflow`; `IsDocumented(Misses, field)` takes the **union** of the three field vocabularies and the XML doc says so.
  - *History:* 2026-09-08 (*amend-docs) BRD-113 amended: the misses dispatch gains a fourth arm, `review` → `MissReviewRecord`; a review record is a fourth kind, not a miss, and must never fall through to `InvalidLines`. 2026-09-09 [REQ-FN-072] **built.** `MissKinds.Review` + `MissReviewRecord` (21 columns, SCHEMA.md §5.5.9); `StreamParser.AddMissRecord` gains `case MissKinds.Review` → `BuildMissReview`; `MissReviewDocumented` (11 wire fields, the last six emitter-derived and copied verbatim) joins `MissesKnown`, so `IsDocumented(Misses, …)` now answers over four vocabularies and no review field is reported as undocumented. The default arm is untouched and still counts-and-skips. `ParseResult.MissReviews` + `RecordCount` follow. **Smoked end to end on live data, no synthetic fixture:** app booted Release on :5111 against a fresh PostgreSQL 16; signed in as `tflensdemo@techierathore.com`; connected `techierathore/TfLens` through `/repos` → `/repos/add` and synced it. Its `docs/metrics/misses.jsonl` at `d1c44b3` carries 86 `miss` + 75 `miss-fix` + **1 `review`** = 162 lines; the store holds `Miss` 86, `MissFix` 75, `MissReview` **1**, and `SyncState.MissesCount` **162** — every line accounted for, none invalid, and the review counted on the stream row without ever becoming a miss. Coverage renders no review field as undocumented. 9 tests in `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Parsing/MissReviewParserTests.cs`.

<a id="d-req-fn-073"></a>
- **REQ-FN-073** — Dedupe per user and repo: `miss` on `(MissId)` keeping the **earliest** `Ts`; `miss-fix` on `(MissId, FixRunId)` keeping the latest; `miss-amend` on `(MissId, Field, Ts)` keeping the earliest (BRD-114).
  - *Acceptance:* When an archived miss file is re-parsed from `/repos`, then each record kind dedupes on its natural key and nothing is double-inserted.
  - *Checks:* earliest-wins for `miss`, latest-wins for `miss-fix`, earliest-wins per `(MissId, Field)` for `miss-amend`; a re-parse of the same archived file double-inserts nothing; no `merge=union` handling is applied — misses are single-machine events and cannot be independently reconstructed elsewhere.
  - *History:* 2026-09-08 (*amend-docs) BRD-114 amended: `review` dedupes on `(UserId, Repo, ReviewPhase, COALESCE(ProducedRunId,''))` earliest-wins; the COALESCE is not optional. 2026-09-09 [REQ-FN-073] **built.** `Dedupe.MissReviews` uses `KeepBest` with `aR.ProducedRunId ?? string.Empty` in the key and an earliest-`Ts` comparator, mirroring `UcMissReviewUserRepoPhaseProduced` exactly — the same two-spellings-of-one-decision rule the other three rules already follow. A record carrying no `phase` has no natural key and is kept, as a commit with no SHA is. **The COALESCE is proven rather than asserted:** both the live record and one of the two fixture records carry a **null** `reviewed_run_id`, and `MissUpsertIsIdempotent` stores the fixture twice against real PostgreSQL 16 for `0` rows on the second pass — a nullable left in the index would have inserted it again. 6 tests added to `MissDedupeTests` (earliest-wins in both arrival orders, the null-COALESCE collapse, one row per phase, one row per produce run, repo scoping, no-key-is-kept).

<a id="d-req-fn-074"></a>
- **REQ-FN-074** — Three tables (`Miss`, `MissFix`, `MissAmend`) in the existing house style, with the unique and read indexes of Architecture §6, and `DeleteRepoDataAsync` purges **all three** (BRD-115).
  - *Acceptance:* When miss records are stored from `/repos`, then they occupy their own tables and removing a repo purges every one of them.
  - *Checks:* `database/001-schema.sql` stays idempotent at every startup (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, every identifier double-quoted, `UserId` in every unique index); `ITelemetryStore` gains `ReadMissesAsync` / `ReadMissFixesAsync` / `ReadMissAmendsAsync`; **guardrail test — `DeleteRepoDataAsync` leaves zero rows in all three tables**; `RebuildAsync` replays them from `data/raw/` with identical counts.
  - *History:* 2026-09-08 (*amend-docs) BRD-115 amended: three miss tables become four (`MissReview`), and `DeleteRepoDataAsync` must purge all four. 2026-09-09 [REQ-FN-074] **built.** `database/001-schema.sql` gains `MissReview` in the existing house style — `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, every identifier double-quoted, `"UserId"` a real column and part of the unique index — carrying the review phase, the corrections count, the two run ids the record names and the two cost pairs **as the emitter copied them** (`null` means not captured, never a measured zero). It is a sibling of the other three, not a column on `"Miss"`. `PostgresStore.StreamTables` — the **one** list the purge and the rebuild both walk — becomes nine entries, and everything keyed to it follows in the same edit: the `SourceSha` CHECK loop in the DDL, `StreamCoverageSql`, `OverflowKeysSql`, `AboveSchemaV1Sql`, `StoredProvenanceSql` and `RecomputeSyncCountsAsync`. `ITelemetryStore` gains `ReadMissReviewsAsync` (default-implemented, so no fake broke). Guardrails: `ThePurgeCoversAllFourMissTables`, `TheSchemaDeclaresTheFourthMissTable` (asserts the literal `COALESCE("ProducedRunId", '')`), `TheStoreExposesAReadForEachMissKind` over four names, and `StoredProvenanceIsRealTests` at nine tables. Against the real database: `DeleteRepoDataLeavesZeroRowsInAllFourMissTables` reads the counts straight from the tables (bypassing the `"UserRepo"` join, so a purge that left rows but broke the join could not pass by looking empty), and `RebuildReplaysTheMissStreamWithIdenticalCounts` now compares a four-tuple. Live: the fourth table exists and holds the real review row; `"Miss"` gains `"Sort"` and `"What"` as guarded nullable ALTERs, so an established database migrates on the next startup. ⚠ **Three deliberate divergences from the Architecture §6 sketch of `"MissReview"`, for the architect to reconcile.** (1) The sketch gives the table an `"Id" bigserial PRIMARY KEY` and types `"UserId"` as `text` and `"Ts"` as `timestamptz`; no other stream table has a surrogate key, and all four siblings type `"UserId"` `integer` and `"Ts"` `text` — ADR-013 and the file's own header rule. The implementation follows the siblings, because a fifth stream table shaped unlike the other four is how a join or a rebuild quietly diverges later. (2) The sketch names the two token columns `"ProducedCostTokens"` / `"CorrectionCostTokens"`; the implementation uses `"TokensProduce"` / `"TokensCorrect"`, the straight PascalCase of the wire names `tokens_produce` / `tokens_correct`, which is the mapping rule every other column in the schema follows. (3) The sketch omits `cost_produce_usd`, `cost_correct_usd`, `model_produce`, `model_correct` and `what`; BRD-115 requires *the two cost figures as the emitter copied them*, and SCHEMA §5.5.9 lists the USD figures separately from the token counts, so tokens alone would not satisfy the clause — all five are implemented. The unique index **was** renamed to the Architecture's `UcMissReviewUserRepoPhaseRunId`. Nothing here changes a figure; it is a naming and house-style reconciliation.

<a id="d-req-fn-075"></a>
- **REQ-FN-075** — Amendments are **stored, not collapsed**, and folded into their parent at read time, oldest first, re-applying the null-check while folding; only allowlisted fields with values in their closed vocabulary are applied; an amend naming no known `miss`, or a field off the allowlist, is an orphan — counted and surfaced on Coverage, never applied (BRD-116, ADR-020).
  - *Acceptance:* When miss amendments are read on `/misses`, then they are folded oldest-first into a null field only, and a rebuild re-derives identical values.
  - *Checks:* an amend fills a `null` ✓ · an amend **never** overwrites a non-`null` value, **whichever order the two records arrive in** ✓ · a second amend of the same field is ignored ✓ · a field off the allowlist or a value outside its vocabulary is never applied and counts as an orphan ✓ · an amend naming no known `miss` counts as an orphan ✓ · a `why_missed` supplied only by an amend reaches the failed-practice distribution ✓ · `RebuildAsync` re-derives identical values because folding never happened at ingest ✓.
  - *History:* 2026-09-08 (*amend-docs) BRD-116 amended: the amend allowlist gains `sort` (closed vocabulary) and `what` (free prose); an out-of-vocabulary `sort` is an orphan, counted and surfaced, never coerced. 2026-09-09 [REQ-FN-075] **built.** `MissAmendFolder.AmendableFields` gains `sort` with its four values; `what` goes into a **separate** `AmendableFreeTextFields` set rather than an empty vocabulary, because an empty list already means *no legal value* and reading it as *every value is legal* is the one mistake that would turn the allowlist into a free-text back door for every field at once (SCHEMA.md §9 constraint 7). `Reject` therefore has two arms: closed vocabulary, or non-empty text. `Current` and `Apply` gain both fields, and `MissRecord` gains `Sort` / `What` (with the parser mapping and the two DDL columns) so the fold has something to write to. Everything else is unchanged: still read-time only, still oldest-first, still re-applying the null-check, still never mutating a stored row. Orphan reasons and counts are unchanged, so Coverage surfaces the new refusals with no page work. **14 tests added to `MissAmendFolderTests`** — the four legal `sort` values, an out-of-vocabulary `sort` refused *and its value shown to the reader* rather than coerced, never overwriting an existing `sort` in either arrival order, free prose accepted, blank prose refused, free text not leaking to `severity`, the three fields folding independently in one pass, a second `sort` amendment *ignored* rather than orphaned, and a re-fold re-deriving identical values. ⚠ Divergence from the framework, logged as **TF-018** (minor, nothing blocked): `tf-emit.sh` refuses to *write* a `what` amendment, and SCHEMA.md §5.5.7 says closed vocabularies only. TfLens follows the owner's amended BRD and reads it.

<a id="d-req-fn-076"></a>
- **REQ-FN-076** — `FIELD_SINCE = { "why_missed": 2026-08-28 }` sits beside the existing `LATE_GATES` table — one table, one code path where possible — and a miss written before the field existed leaves that field's denominator, reported separately as `why_missed_eligible` and `why_missed_predates_field` (BRD-117).
  - *Acceptance:* When a miss predating an optional field is read on `/misses`, then it leaves that field's denominator and is reported separately.
  - *Checks:* a miss with `Ts` before the floor is outside the denominator and is counted separately — the same shape as the existing late-gate test for `perf`; both counts appear in the export and match parity, so `n of N assessed` cannot disagree with the oracle on a repo holding pre-2026-08-28 misses.
  - *History:* 2026-09-08 (*amend-docs) BRD-117 amended: `FIELD_SINCE` gains `sort` and `what`, both 2026-09-07, reported as `<field>_predates_field`. 2026-09-09 [REQ-FN-076] **built.** Two rows added to `MetricsConstants.FieldSince`; no new code path, which is the point — the floor is declared once, in data, and every figure built on either field inherits it through the single `LateGateCoverageCalculator.EligibilityFor`. `MetricsConstants.EligibleKey` / `PredatesFieldKey` put the `<field>_eligible` / `<field>_predates_field` spelling in one place so a reader checking `n of N` against the oracle finds the same two keys whichever field they read. **A correctness defect was found and fixed while doing this:** `EligibilityFor` excluded a pre-floor record even when it *carried* the field. For `why_missed` that never fired; for `sort` it would have fired constantly, because BRD-116's whole purpose is completing `sort` on records written before the field existed — the fold would have added the answer and the floor would have thrown it straight back out, leaving the amendment with no visible effect on any figure. The reference resolves it the same way (`_eligible`'s `or bool(rec.get(field))`, *"an older record completed by an amend is sorted, whatever its date"*), so the two now agree by construction rather than by luck. `IsEligibleForField` deliberately keeps the old shape and says why: its callers ask about records *missing* the field, where the escape could never change an answer. **9 tests added to `MissFieldEligibilityTests`**, including the fold-then-floor round trip that would have caught the defect.

<a id="d-req-fn-077"></a>

### Phase 3 — the 2026-09-07 stream fields and three reader-side corrections (added 2026-09-08)

*BRD-178, BRD-179 and BRD-180 are **corrections, not enhancements** — three sums TfLens computes today, each wrong in the direction that flatters the figure. Each was a real defect in the framework's own report before it was fixed there.*

<a id="d-req-fn-106"></a>
- **REQ-FN-106** — Parse and store `sort` on a `miss` record in its closed four-value vocabulary — `spec`, `unsaid`, `weak-check`, `ignored` (BRD-170).
  - *Acceptance:* When a miss carries a sort value outside the four on `/`, then the value is shown as unrecognised and counted, never filed under the nearest legal one.
  - *Checks:* When a miss carries a `sort` value outside the four on `/`, then the value is shown as unrecognised and counted, never filed under the nearest legal one.
  - *History:* 2026-09-09 `[REQ-FN-106]` **BUILT.** The four values are declared **once**, as `MissSorts` in `src/TfLens.Core/Contracts/StreamRecords.cs`, and `MissAmendFolder.AmendableFields["sort"]` now **is that same list** rather than a second copy of it — a guardrail test asserts reference identity (`Assert.Same`), because two lists that agree today are two places for a closed set to stop being closed, and the drift would surface as misses filed under the wrong remedy. A stored value outside the four is kept exactly as it arrived: `MissFigures.SortRows` gives it its own row on `MissSegmentFigures.SortDistribution` carrying `MissCategoryCount.IsRecognised = false`, and `SortUnrecognised` counts it. The flag travels **with the row** rather than being recomputed by whoever renders it, so a renderer cannot forget it. It stays in the numerator — a record that answered is a record that answered, just not with one of the four — which is also what the reference's own `Counter` does. Nothing coerces and nothing filters: coercion hides a producer's disagreement inside a figure nobody can audit, and filtering leaves totals that look entirely normal while records go missing. Nothing was rewritten: `MissRecord.Sort`, the parse mapping and the amend allowlist landed with the concurrent cluster and are **bound**, not re-implemented. Tests: `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Metrics/MissSortFiguresTests.cs` (13 tests — a near-miss `weak-checks` keeps its own row while the legal `weak-check` row still counts 1; blank is absence and never an unrecognised value; an amend outside the four is an orphan and the parent's `Sort` stays `null`) and two new guardrail facts in `tests/TfLens.Guardrails.Tests/MissInvariantContractTests.cs`. Smoke evidence in the REQ-FN-107 entry below.
<a id="d-req-fn-107"></a>
- **REQ-FN-107** — The denominator of every `sort` figure is the misses **eligible** to carry it, via `FIELD_SINCE` (BRD-172, BRD-117).
  - *Acceptance:* When a user reads the sort distribution on `/misses`, then its denominator counts only eligible misses and records predating the field are stated apart.
  - *Checks:* When the owner reads the `sort` distribution on `/misses`, then its denominator counts only eligible misses and records predating the field are stated apart.
  - *History:* 2026-09-09 `[REQ-FN-107]` **BUILT.** `MissSegmentFigures.SortEligibility` comes from the **one existing code path**, `LateGateCoverageCalculator.EligibilityFor`, which reads the 2026-09-07 floor out of `MetricsConstants.FieldSince` — the floor is declared in data, once, and nothing here re-derives the comparison. `FieldEligibility` gained two members that make the wrong number unwritable rather than merely discouraged: `NOfN(participle)` builds the phrase from `Eligible` so a caller cannot build it from the miss count, and it deliberately leaves `PredatesField` out of that sentence because pooling the two is the failure the rule names; `NoneFor(field)` gives an empty block the field's real `since` date rather than implying the field always existed. The type still carries no rate. The export gained the reference's own three keys — `sort_n`, `sort_eligible`, `sort_predates_field` — plus the `sort` distribution, written in `SnapshotJson.Misses` beside `why_missed`'s trio; `tools/parity-compare.py` lists all four in `MISSES_KEYS`, so their coverage is a **checked fact** rather than a happy accident and a key vanishing from either side fails the run. **Smoke (2026-09-09, headless Playwright against the app booted on :5137 over the configured `WinPostgre localhost:5550/tflens`, signed in as `tflensdemo@techierathore.com`):** a probe stream imported through the app's own **Import metric files** journey — four legally sorted misses, one carrying `weak-checks`, one after-floor unsorted, two before-floor, one before-floor completed by a `miss-amend`, and two `review` records. The band reads **6 of 7 sorted** with *"91 misses predate the field (2026-09-07) — written before sort existed, and reported here rather than counted as records that declined to answer"*. A denominator over all misses would have read **6 of 98**; that is the whole margin this row exists to remove. `tests/verify/fn-miss-sort-stream.spec.ts` **7 passed** (56.3s), visual gate clean at 1280 and 390 with page-pane overflow 0px and **0 console errors**, screenshots `tests/.artifacts/misses/sort-band-{1280,390}.png`; the probe source is removed through the app's own Remove path in the last test and the estate verified back to its prior repositories. Unit tests: `MissSortFiguresTests` (the denominator is the eligible misses, not the miss count; predating is its own count and never the unanswered one; an older record completed by an amend is *sorted whatever its date*, or the fold would fold an answer in and immediately throw it away) and `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Export/MissExportTests.cs` (`sort_eligible` 0 / `sort_predates_field` 4 over a fixture dated entirely before the floor — the export must not say those four declined to answer).

  - *History:* 2026-09-09 `[REQ-FN-108]` **VERIFIED BY TEST, NOT REWRITTEN.** The ingest half already landed with the concurrent cluster and was read before a line was written: `MissKinds.Review`, `MissReviewRecord`, the `"MissReview"` table, `Dedupe.MissReviews` on `(ReviewPhase, ProducedRunId)`, the fourth dispatch arm in `StreamParser.AddMissRecord` and `ReadMissReviewsAsync` are all present and covered by `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Parsing/MissReviewParserTests.cs`. What was genuinely missing was the **read-side** guarantee, and fixing the parse created the hazard: a kind the reader now *knows* is a kind the reader could start counting. It is now structural rather than a filter — `MissFigures.Compute` has **no parameter** a `MissReviewRecord` could arrive through, and no miss result type (`MissAnalysis`, `MissSegmentFigures`, `MissFoldResult`) has a property that could hold one; both are asserted by reflection, so there is no code path to get wrong rather than a rule to remember. New: `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Metrics/MissReviewIsNeverAMissTests.cs` (6 tests) walks a six-record mixed stream end to end — two misses, a fix, an amend and two reviews — and pins **0 `InvalidLines`**, a miss count of 2, no distribution row anywhere keyed by a review phase, no review reaching a period or segment filter, and the two reviews counted **apart** on the Coverage diagnostics (`MissStreamDiagnostics.ReviewRecords` = 2 beside `misses_total` = 2 — two facts, never one). Smoke: the probe's `build-review` and `verify-review` records render their own rows on `/misses` and appear nowhere in the whose-gap table or its denominator, which still reads `of 7 sorted`. ⚠ carried, not built: the reference also emits `reviews_n`, `review_corrections`, `reviews_by_phase` and the two `review_tokens_*` pairs in its `misses` block and TfLens's export does not yet — logged below as a gap for the export/parity row rather than added here, since BRD-174's clause is the negative one and is met.
<a id="d-req-fn-109"></a>
- **REQ-FN-109** — Any distribution computed over folded records states how many values were completed by a `miss-amend` (BRD-176).
  - *Acceptance:* When a distribution over folded records renders on `/misses`, then the page states how many values an amendment completed.
  - *Checks:* When a distribution over folded records renders on `/misses`, then the page states how many values an amendment completed.
  - *History:* 2026-09-09 `[REQ-FN-109]` **BUILT.** The fold now records **what it completed**, not merely how much: `MissFoldResult` carries `Completions` — a `MissAmendCompletion(Repo, MissId, Field)` per applied amendment — and `AmendmentsApplied` is *derived* from it, so the published count cannot drift from the list it is supposed to summarise. `MissSegmentFigures.AmendedValues` publishes one entry per name in `MissFigures.DistributionFields` (`miss_class`, `why_missed`, `sort`, `found_by`), counted over **the segment's own misses** — a total spanning every project type attached to a distribution that spans one would be the same category error the segmentation exists to prevent. A field no amendment may complete reports `0` rather than being absent: "no amendment touched this" is an answer and a missing key is not. The guardrail test drives its expectation from the **same constant the figures do**, so a distribution added later cannot ship without its count — which is precisely the failure the clause names, one refactor downstream. Tests: `MissSortFiguresTests` (every distribution has a count beside it; an amended count stays in its own segment — a `library` miss's amendment never lands on the `app` band; an orphan amend completes nothing and `CompletedFor("sort")` stays 0) and the guardrail fact above. Smoke: `/misses` states *"1 of the answers arrived later, by a miss-amend folded in at read time. The stored miss rows still carry null — an amend completes a record and never edits it."* over the probe's one amended record.

- **REQ-FN-112** — `duration_s` is taken from the record's own timestamps at read time, and they override a stored figure that disagrees (BRD-179, amended 2026-09-10).
  - *Acceptance:* When a user reads a run on `/effort` whose stored `duration_s` disagrees with its own `started` and `ended` by more than a second, then the timestamps are used, the record counts as recomputed, and a run whose `ended` precedes its `started` contributes no duration at all.
  - *Checks:* When the owner reads a run on `/effort` whose stored `duration_s` disagrees with its own `started` and `ended` by more than a second, then the timestamps are used and the record counts as recomputed; a run whose `ended` precedes its `started` is excluded from every duration figure and counted as impossible; a run that starts and ends in the same second is counted as recording no elapsed time and is never reported as corrupt.
  - *Amended 2026-09-10:* the rule this was verified against on 2026-09-09 has changed. The old rule derived a duration only where the record **omitted** one and returned a record carrying a duration untouched; the reference of 2026-09-09 lets the **timestamps win** over a stored figure that disagrees with them, and excludes an impossible record instead of admitting it. Set to `Needs re-verify`; the history below records what was built against the old rule.
  - *History:* 2026-09-09 `[REQ-FN-112]` new `TfLens.Core/Metrics/RunDuration.cs` + `PhaseDuration.DerivedN` + `PhaseEffortAnalysis.DurationsDerivedN`. `PhaseMetrics.Compute` derives before it groups, so the phase's time and its tokens finally cover the same set of runs — under the bug a run carrying both timestamps but no `duration_s` was worth **zero time while its tokens still counted** (the framework's reset: 16h49m reported against a true 55h57m). The derivation is `ended − started`, with `ts` standing in for an absent `ended` per SCHEMA.md's own definition; it refuses a run with no `started`, an unparseable pair or a negative window rather than clamping, and it skips backfilled runs because a reconstructed duration is a guess. Nothing is written back: `RunRecord.DurationDerivedFrom` is a read-time annotation no wire field maps to, no column holds and no insert names. The count rides on the block so a surface cannot render the total without it — `/effort` states it on the wall-clock tile (`data-testid="kpi-wallclock-derived"`) and in each phase's timed-runs note. The export's `phases.<cmd>.duration_s.derived_n` is a key **the oracle emits too**, so it went into `PHASES_NESTED_KEYS` in `tools/parity-compare.py` as required-on-both-sides rather than into `ADDED_KEYS`; `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Fixtures/Engine/reference.json` was regenerated by `make-reference.sh` (the oracle itself, never hand-written) and all three `MetricsEngineParityTests` pass against it. **Smoked 2026-09-09** on the live app against the configured `localhost:5550/tflens` (`WinPostgre`), signed in as `tflensdemo@techierathore.com` (the canonical user, `docs/TfLens-UsageGuide.md`): `tests/verify/cluster-c2-reader-corrections.spec.ts` **2 passed**, 0 console errors, 0 overlap or off-viewport findings at 1280 and 390 over `/`, `/gate-outcomes` and `/effort`. What the screens actually read: tabs `app 802 · docs 225 · framework requirements (req_class FR) 41 · library 24` — **all 41 FR verdicts in their own tab and none in an application one**; `live: 802 records, 333 REQs scored, 0 excluded by backfill taint, **35 attempts derived**`; combined first-pass rate **48%** — the figure BRD-178 says a correctly keyed rollup produces where the bare-id one read 72%; `/effort` wall clock `198h 18m · Σ duration_s over 142 timed runs · **7 derived from started/ended**` — all seven duration-less runs answered, six from `ended` and one from `ts`.
  - *Build 2026-09-11 [REQ-FN-112]:* **Root cause of "62 of 36 timed durations were derived":** the page printed the reference's per-phase `derived_n` as a part of the timed runs, but `derived_n` also counts every run closed from its own timestamps that started and ended in the same second — 62 `log-miss` runs derived, none of them timed. The export key is correct and unchanged (it is the reference's own); the page sentence was wrong. **Fix:** `RunDuration.Derive` now also marks each record `recomputed`, `impossible` or `absent` (`RunRecord.DurationQuality`, read-time only, never stored); `PhaseDuration` gains `DerivedTimedN` (derived **and** timed, never more than the timed runs), `RecomputedN`, `ImpossibleN` and `AbsentN` per phase; `PhaseEffortAnalysis.DurationsDerivedTimedN` is the page-level sum. `/effort` states them beside every time figure — wall-clock tile: `Σ duration_s over 227 timed runs · 2 derived from started/ended · 20 recomputed from the timestamps over a stored duration_s that disagreed · excluded, never counted as zero: 15 with an end before the start, 71 with no elapsed time`; each phase's time band: e.g. `log-miss` `Timed on 36 of 107 runs. Excluded, carrying no duration: 0 with an end before the start, 71 with no elapsed time. 8 recomputed …` (no run is called corrupt, impossible or failing). Tests: `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Metrics/PhaseDurationCountsTests.cs` (derived-beside-time never exceeds timed; recomputed and end-before-start counted on their own phase; per-phase timed + end-before-start + no-elapsed = runs and sums to the page counts). Smoked on a private instance as the demo user: every phase satisfies timed + exclusions = runs, 0 overlap at 1280/390; the REQ-FN-112 verifier test passes with page and export agreeing (313 runs; measured 227, impossible 15, absent 71, recomputed 20).

The rule is SCHEMA.md §3.1's *definition*, not a judgement: one plus the prior **live** records for the same `(project, req_id)`, walked in the oracle's order (`ts` then `run_id`, both ordinal, so the numbering depends on the data rather than on the order the store returned rows in). <a id="d-req-fn-088"></a>
- **REQ-FN-088** — The three SCHEMA §2.6 fields added to `runs.jsonl` on 2026-08-31 — `subagent_runs`, `tokens_out_subagents`, `model_tokens_out` — are parsed and stored as **nullable by design**, and a `runs` record carrying a field the parser does not know goes to `Overflow` rather than incrementing `InvalidLines` (BRD-145, BRD-29).
  - *Acceptance:* When a run record is parsed from `/repos`, then the three fan-out fields are stored nullable and an unknown field goes to overflow, never invalid lines.
  - *Checks:* a fixture run with all three fields absent stores three `NULL`s and a fixture run with `subagent_runs: 0` stores a zero, and a test asserts the two are distinguishable at the store level (the existing `StoreNullVsZero` discipline); `ModelTokensOut` round-trips a two-model split without loss; a record carrying an unrecognised `v=1` property parses successfully, lands the property in `Overflow`, and leaves `InvalidLines` at 0; `RebuildAsync` re-derives identical values from `data/raw/`.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). `null` means *not captured*; a measured zero is a different fact. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-088]` built: `RunRecord` gains `SubagentRuns` (`int?`), `TokensOutSubagents` (`int?`) and `ModelTokensOut` (`IReadOnlyDictionary<string,long>?`, `jsonb` — read whole, never joined, per ADR-025's contrast note); the three wire names joined `RunDocumented` in `StreamParser` so they resolve to columns and never surface as undocumented on Coverage; `"Run"` gained the three guarded `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` columns plus `IxRunUserCmd`. Raw rows only — nothing coalesces at ingest, so `RebuildAsync` re-derives the same nulls from the stream. `JsonMapTypeHandler` (new) round-trips the map through Dapper. Smoked against the real PostgreSQL: a `tree`-scope line stores 3 / 610 / `{opus:900, haiku:120}` and a `main`-scope line reads back three nulls, not zeros. An unknown producer field lands in `Overflow` with `InvalidLines` still 0. 9 new tests. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-088] BUILT + VERIFIED.** The three SCHEMA §2.6 fields land on `RunRecord` as `SubagentRuns` (`int?`), `TokensOutSubagents` (`int?`) and `ModelTokensOut` (`IReadOnlyDictionary<string,long>?` — the codebase's read-only collection idiom, semantically the brief's nullable map), parsed, stored and replayable. `ModelTokensOut` is `jsonb` and **not** a child table, deliberately: it is a per-model split read only ever as a whole, and ADR-025 makes that the rule — a split that must be QUERIED becomes a table (which is why `PbPhaseModelUsage` is one). A new `JsonMapTypeHandler` was needed because it is the only structured non-overflow `jsonb` column and Dapper cannot read one without a handler; `NULL` is preserved both ways and never becomes `"{}"`. An unknown producer field goes to `Overflow` and does not touch `InvalidLines` — the producer added fields in August and again now, and will again. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). This is `TF-005` on a third stream; the error always flatters the framework. **Built 2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-089]** — `PhaseMetrics` partitions each phase's runs into priced and unpriced (`tokens_scope` `none`/absent, or no `tokens_out`) and returns every derived token figure as `TokenWindow(Figure Tokens, int MeasuredN, int UnmeasuredN)`, so a page cannot bind the number without its denominator; `tokens_out_per_run` is `InsufficientData(n)` below `MIN_N`, never `0`. Verified against the live oracle over this repo's own 44 live runs: 401 keys, 0 diffs. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-089] BUILT + VERIFIED.** A run with no usable `tokens_scope` leaves every token figure and is counted as `tokens_unmeasured_n` — **never averaged in as zero**. `tokens_out_per_run` is `InsufficientData` below `MIN_N = 3`, never `0`. The figure arrives wrapped as `TokenWindow(Figure Tokens, int MeasuredN, int UnmeasuredN)`, so a page cannot render the total without also holding its denominator — the wrong number is unrepresentable rather than forbidden. - **REQ-FN-090** — Fan-out figures are computed only over runs with `tokens_scope == "tree"` **and** a non-null `subagent_runs`, and the excluded runs are classified and counted two ways: `unobserved_not_tree` and `unobserved_predates_field` (BRD-147, ADR-026).
  - *Acceptance:* When a fan-out figure is read on `/effort`, then only tree-scope runs with a recorded count contribute, and the two exclusions stay two counts.
  - *Checks:* a dataset of 13 runs with 1 tree-scope-and-populated, 2 main-scope and 10 pre-2026-08-31 reports `observed_n = 1`, `unobserved_not_tree = 2`, `unobserved_predates_field = 10`, and **never** a spawn count of 0 for the other 12; a phase with `observed_n == 0` renders the literal text "not observed" and no numeral; a test asserts no code path applies `?? 0` or `GetValueOrDefault()` to `SubagentRuns`; the returned `FanoutObservation` has no property exposing a spawn count without its `ObservedN`.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). A predicate, never a coalesce — `?? 0` turns "we did not look" into a measurement. **Built 2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-090]** — `FanoutObservation(double? Spawns, int ObservedN, int UnobservedNotTree, int UnobservedPredatesField)`; the predicate is `tree` scope **and** non-null `subagent_runs`, and there is no `?? 0` on any §2.6 field (REQ-NFR-023 guardrail green). `Spawns`/`SpawnsMax` are nullable numbers rather than `Figure`s because `analyse_phases` applies no `MIN_N` floor to them — unlike its pooled block — and a floor here would refuse a number the oracle prints. Architecture §7's table says `Figure` for these three; it needs amending to match the oracle. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-090] BUILT + VERIFIED.** Fan-out is a **predicate**, not a coalesce: `TokensScope == "tree"` **and** `SubagentRuns is not null`. The two exclusions are published as two counts because they are two facts with different futures — `unobserved_not_tree` (*we did not look*; could change tomorrow) and `unobserved_predates_field` (*we could not have looked*; never will). `FanoutObservation` carries `ObservedN` beside the figure. **A correction to the amended docs, resolved in the shipped oracle's favour** (BRD §13 is zero-tolerance): `analyse_phases` derives `unobserved_predates_field` from *tree scope with a null `subagent_runs`* and **does not consult a date** — `FIELD_SINCE` is cited as why that inference holds, not as the test. Upsert is `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE … WHERE (payload excluding ImportedAt) IS DISTINCT FROM EXCLUDED`: an identical re-import writes **0 rows**, while an EOF window later read as closed genuinely replaces the stored row. <a id="d-req-fn-096"></a>
- **REQ-FN-096** — Rows failing the producer's invariants, carrying `data_quality.valid:false`, or containing no finalized assistant turn are quarantined: stored, displayed with their reason, and excluded from every numeric aggregate (BRD-155).
  - *Acceptance:* When an invalid or incomplete schema-2 row is read on `/effort`, then it is quarantined with its reason and enters no numeric aggregate.
  - *Checks:* a row whose `tokens_in != input + cache_read + cache_write` is quarantined; a row with `subagents.spawned < contributors` is quarantined; a row with `observed_active_ms > elapsed_ms` on a complete window is quarantined; a `complete:false` row that carries a non-null `ended_at` or `elapsed_ms` is quarantined; a start/end window with **no assistant turn** is quarantined rather than displayed as a free run; a quarantined row carrying zero-valued compatibility totals contributes **nothing** to any total — asserted by comparing an aggregate computed with and without it; every quarantined row is visible in the execution table with its `data_quality.issues` rendered.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). Must happen **before** aggregation: an invalid row's retained zero is indistinguishable from a real one afterwards. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-096]` built: `src/TfLens.Core/Playbook/PlaybookPhaseInvariants.cs` checks all five §3.1 invariants — the two token compatibility sums, `spawned >= contributors`, `0 <= observed_active_ms <= elapsed_ms` on a complete window, and `complete:false ⇒ end_reason "eof"` with null end and elapsed — plus `data_quality.valid:false` and **no finalized assistant turn**, each with a stable code and a sentence. It is a **pure function of the stored row, re-run at read time**: `PlaybookPhaseReport.Build` validates every row before it forms a single cohort, so no caller depends on a stored flag and a row written by an older build is judged by today's rules. At ingest the findings are also written into `DataQualityIssues` (producer issues verbatim first, TfLens codes prefixed `tflens:`) so a stored row can state its own reason. Nothing is repaired — no leg recomputed, no boundary inferred. An invariant whose inputs are absent is deliberately **not** a violation, or sparse-but-honest data would be quarantined. Tests: each invariant violated one at a time quarantines; a quarantined row carrying zero-valued compatibility totals leaves every total byte-identical to the same report without it, while staying visible with its reasons. Not 100%: rendering the reason in the execution table is REQ-UI-050's half. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-096] BUILT + VERIFIED.** All five producer invariants are checked, and quarantine is a **pure function re-run at read time before any cohort is formed** — the findings are also written onto the stored row so it states its own reason, but nothing downstream trusts a stored verdict. This ordering is the whole point: the producer may retain zero-valued compatibility totals on an invalid row, so a consumer that aggregates first gets a confident zero rather than an error. Quarantined rows stay visible with their reason; nothing is silently repaired. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). <a id="d-req-fn-097"></a>
- **REQ-FN-097** — Wall-clock elapsed, observed active time and human effort are kept as three separate concepts, and the diagnostic component sums are never added (BRD-156, ADR-027).
  - *Acceptance:* When timing figures are read on `/effort`, then wall clock, observed active time and human effort stay distinct and the diagnostic sums are never added.
  - *Checks:* overlapping assistant, tool and child intervals are counted **once** via the producer's union — a fixture with two parallel subagents active in the same minute contributes one busy minute, not two; a test asserts no code path computes `AssistantElapsedMs + ToolElapsedMs`; `coverage:"partial"` renders as an explicit lower bound and `unavailable` renders no figure; no label, column header, tooltip or export key describes observed active time as human effort, CPU time, utilization or additive compute; there is no human-effort column, property or key anywhere.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). Active time is never labelled human effort, CPU time or utilization. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-097]` built: `ObservedActiveMs` (the producer's **union**, overlaps counted once) and `ElapsedMs` are two separate members of `PhaseExecutionView`, and `assistant_elapsed_ms` / `tool_elapsed_ms` are published as `PhaseDiagnostic` — a record whose only members are strings, with **no numeric property and no `op_Addition`** — so their sum is a compile-time absence rather than a rule someone has to remember (ADR-027). A reflection test pins that shape and a second scans every public member of `TfLens.Core` for `HumanEffort` / `CpuTime` / `CpuMs` / `Utilization`, of which there are none: human effort has no column, no property and no export key anywhere. `coverage:"partial"` renders `at least …` with the standing lower-bound sentence; `unavailable` renders **no figure at all** and enters no comparison; both rows stay visible in the table. Not 100%: the column headers and tooltips that must never relabel it are REQ-UI-050's half. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-097] BUILT + VERIFIED — and the wrong sum is a compile-time absence, not a rule.** `assistant_elapsed_ms` and `tool_elapsed_ms` are `PhaseDiagnostic` values carrying **strings only** — no numeric member and no `op_Addition` — so `assistant + tool` cannot be written, which is exactly the number a well-meaning contributor would compute because both columns are right there and adding them looks like totalling. `ObservedActiveMs` is the producer's **union**, overlaps counted once. A reflection test proves no `HumanEffort` / `CpuTime` / `Utilization` member exists anywhere in `TfLens.Core`: human effort gets no column at all, because a column that exists is one something eventually populates by inference from wall-clock. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). <a id="d-req-fn-100"></a>
- **REQ-FN-100** — Subagent fan-out is reported as `contributors / spawned`, recursive children are counted exactly once, child usage is never re-summed onto phase totals, the difference is never an inferred failure, and an absent agent type is never inferred (BRD-159).
  - *Acceptance:* When subagent fan-out is read on `/effort`, then it shows contributors over spawned and a recursive child is counted exactly once.
  - *Checks:* three spawned children with one token contributor display `1 / 3` and **all three** appear in the detail; a recursive grandchild renders beneath its parent and is included in the phase total exactly once; a test asserts phase totals are not increased by adding child rows (they are already included); the `spawned − contributors` difference is labelled *zero-token / non-contributing child* in the rendered copy and nowhere called a failure; a child with no `agent` renders **unavailable** and no code infers one from a title or a model name; child token share is computed only where `tokens_out > 0`.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-100]` built: `PhaseFanoutView` renders `contributors / spawned` (`1 / 3` for three children with one contributor) and exposes the difference only through `NonContributingLabel` = "zero-token / non-contributing child" — the word *failure* appears nowhere and a test asserts it. The adapter flattens `subagents.sessions[]` **recursively**, keyed on `(execution, session_id)` with keep-last, so a grandchild that arrives both nested and in the flat list is stored once; `PhaseSubagentNode` rebuilds the tree from `ParentSessionId` and a parent naming a session no row reports stays a root rather than being dropped. Child rows are a drill-down: a test proves adding them leaves the phase token totals byte-identical, because their usage is already inside the phase window. An absent `agent` renders `unavailable` and is never inferred from a title or a model name; child token share is computed only where `tokens_out > 0`, and is `unavailable` otherwise. The report-level fan-out figures are restricted to `tokens_scope:"tree"` windows and carry the excluded count beside them — a window that never read the sub-agent transcripts did not report zero (ADR-026). Not 100%: the fan-out chart and the expandable tree are REQ-UI-050's half. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-100] BUILT + VERIFIED.** Fan-out reads `contributors / spawned`; a recursive grandchild renders beneath its parent and is counted in the phase total **exactly once**; child usage is never re-summed onto phase totals because it is already included. `spawned − contributors` is described as a *zero-token or non-contributing child* and **never as an inferred failure**, and an absent child agent type displays **unavailable** rather than being inferred as `"unknown"` from a title or a model name. Child share is computed only where the denominator is positive. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). - **REQ-FN-101** — Provider cost statuses are honoured and measured and estimated dollars never mix (BRD-160).
  - *Acceptance:* When provider cost is read on `/effort`, then a zero-unverified cost is excluded with its status and never shown as free or measured zero.

The repo-wide `PhaseEffortIntegrityTests` guardrail (119/119) scans this code for an estimate pooled with measured spend and for a rate card on an effort surface. A phase whose models do not all report `cost_status:"complete"` is **partial**, not complete. Measured `cost_usd` shares no series, total or aggregate with a rate-card estimate anywhere — page, API or export. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). <a id="d-req-fn-102"></a>
- **REQ-FN-102** — The producer's aggregation cohorts are enforced and each figure states its `n` and exclusions beside it (BRD-161).
  - *Acceptance:* When any cohort figure is read on `/effort`, then its n and exclusions appear beside it, and a cohort under three renders insufficient data.
  - *Checks:* duration aggregates draw only on `complete:true` rows and state the excluded count; active-time averages, percentiles, ratios and model comparisons draw only on `complete:true` **and** `active_coverage:"complete"`, and a partial-coverage execution is **excluded from the comparison chart while remaining visible in the table**; token totals additionally require `data_quality.valid:true` and `token_status:"complete"`; measured-cost totals additionally require `cost_status:"complete"`; `legacy-unverified` rows are reachable by drill-down and absent from schema-2 comparisons; a cohort below three records renders `insufficient data (n=…)`; no copy anywhere claims event-delivery completeness from a token or coverage status; storage and filtering are UTC and only display is localized.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). No producer status is evidence of end-to-end delivery completeness. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-102]` built: four nested cohorts as public predicates — `IsDurationEligible` (closed window), `IsActiveEligible` (+ `active_coverage:"complete"`), `IsTokenEligible` (+ `data_quality.valid:true` and `token_status:"complete"`), `IsMeasuredCostEligible` (+ a complete cost on the phase **and** every contributing model) — each applied only after quarantine. Every figure is a `PhaseFigure` carrying its `n` and a list of `PhaseExclusion`s with per-reason counts, rendered by `Caption` **beside** the figure rather than in a global footer. `PhaseValue.Comparative` refuses to be a number below `MetricsConstants.MinN`, rendering `insufficient data (n=…)`; `legacy-unverified` schema-1 rows are labelled at ingest, stay in the execution table for drill-down and are excluded from every schema-2 total with the count stated. Timestamps are normalized to UTC at ingest and every cohort boundary compares UTC. **Interpretation logged:** the three-record floor is applied to *comparative* figures (median, p90, ratios, model comparisons) and not to sums — a token or cost total is legitimate at n=1 and is published with its n, since suppressing an honest total would hide data rather than protect a comparison. No copy in this cluster claims event-delivery completeness from any status. Not 100%: the chart-level exclusion badges are REQ-UI-050's half. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-102] BUILT + VERIFIED.** The four cohorts are enforced with each one's `n` and exclusions stated **beside the figure**, `legacy-unverified` schema-1 rows are drill-down only, storage and filtering are UTC. **A reading taken and recorded:** `insufficient data (n<3)` is applied to comparative figures — median, p90, ratios, model comparisons — and **not** to sums, because a token or cost total is legitimate at n=1 and is published with its n; suppressing an honest total hides data rather than protecting a comparison. `MISS-TfLens-20260901-10`. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). <a id="d-req-fn-103"></a>
- **REQ-FN-103** — The Playbook's normalized miss export is ingested into the existing `Miss` / `MissFix` / `MissAmend` tables, keyed on an immutable source-line identity preserving stream order, with amendments re-folded at read time and orphan/overwrite diagnostics surfaced (BRD-164, ADR-024).
  - *Acceptance:* When the Playbook miss export is imported on `/repos`, then it lands in the existing miss tables keyed on its own source-line identity.
  - *Checks:* re-importing the same export does not duplicate a lifecycle record; amendments fold **before** any `why_missed` distribution is computed, and the null-check is re-applied on the TfLens side rather than trusted; an amend that would overwrite a non-null value is ignored and counted as an **overwrite diagnostic**, visible on Coverage; an amend naming no known miss is an orphan, counted and never applied; a Playbook row and a TechieFlow row sharing a `MissId` do not collide — the partial unique index keys Playbook rows on `SourceLineHash` and TechieFlow rows on `MissId`; `DeleteRepoDataAsync` purges both editions' rows.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). Partial unique index so the two editions' natural keys coexist without colliding on `NULL`. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-103]` **schema half only — the ingest half is this REQ's other half and belongs to the normalizer cluster.** Built: `SourceLineHash` (nullable) added to `"Miss"`, `"MissFix"` and `"MissAmend"` as guarded ALTERs and to all three C# records and the store's insert paths, plus the partial unique index `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "UcMissUserRepoSourceLine" ON "Miss" ("UserId","Repo","SourceLineHash") WHERE "SourceLineHash" IS NOT NULL;`. The `WHERE` clause is the point and is pinned by a test that also fails on a `COALESCE`. Architecture spells out the index only for `"Miss"`, so `"MissFix"`/`"MissAmend"` get the column without one — `MissFix` already keys on `(MissId, COALESCE(FixRunId,''))` and a second natural key on one table gives a repair two ways to be unique. Smoked against the real PostgreSQL: three TechieFlow misses with `NULL` hashes coexist (4 rows), and re-inserting a different `MissId` on the same hash collapses to the row already stored. **Not built here:** `PlaybookMissNormalizer`, the hash computation, stream-order preservation, read-time amendment re-folding and the orphan/overwrite diagnostics. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-103]` **ingest + read halves now built** (cluster D): `src/TfLens.Core/Playbook/PlaybookMissNormalizer.cs` turns the exporter's stdout into rows for the three existing tables, keyed on `SourceLineHashOf` = SHA-256 of the trimmed source line (lower-case hex), deduped by that hash within a file and left in file order so stream order survives; a malformed line or an unknown `kind` is counted and skipped, never thrown. `data_quality` / `source_window` / `*_usd_estimate` reach no column and are preserved verbatim in `Overflow`, which is where the guards read them back from. `Read`/`ReadAsync` re-fold amendments through the **existing** `MissAmendFolder` — no second folding implementation, per ADR-024 — and publish `PlaybookMissDiagnostics` (applied / overwrite-ignored / orphan amends / orphan fixes) for a UI cluster to bind on Coverage. Smoked: 34 unit tests in `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Playbook/PlaybookMissNormalizerTests.cs` + `PlaybookMissGuardTests.cs`, and 6 live-PostgreSQL tests in `tests/TfLens.Integration.Tests/CrossFrameworkIsolationTests.cs`; full suites 615/615 core, 119/119 guardrails, all green. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-103] BUILT + VERIFIED, ingest included.** The normalizer upserts the exporter's `miss` / `miss-fix` / `miss-amend` rows into the **existing** three tables on an immutable **source-line hash**, preserving stream order, and re-folds valid amendments **at read time** by reusing `MissAmendFolder` — not a second implementation, which is ADR-024's whole argument. Orphan and overwrite diagnostics are exposed for Coverage rather than applied or discarded silently. Cluster A's partial index (`WHERE "SourceLineHash" IS NOT NULL`) lets the two editions' natural keys coexist without colliding on `NULL`. **A gap found by the UI cluster and closed in this pass:** the normalizer had **no caller** — the engine and the surface were both ready and nothing connected the exporter's stdout to them, so BRD-167's populated axis was unreachable by a user. Wired as `playbook-misses.ndjson` → `playbookmisses`, mapped whole-file inside `StreamParser.Parse` (the mapping is whole-file because the rows key on a per-line hash and stream order is part of the record) and written by the same `UpsertAsync`. **The first placement was wrong and an existing guardrail caught it:** the branch went into `TelemetryImportService`, and `NoImportOnlyParseDedupeOrUpsertExists` (REQ-FN-083) failed the build — correctly, since the import module may own no parse path of its own. Moved rather than renamed around. **Spec gap:** the AIFP contract defers its field list to a `Telemetry-Guide.md §7` TfLens does not hold, so the source-window and cost-status spellings are read with a documented fallback in which **absent is never a pass**. `MISS-TfLens-20260901-11`. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). Booted from WSL against the **configured** dev DB (`WinPostgre localhost:5550/tflens`, user secrets, no override). Playwright **95 passed / 3 failed / 3 skipped of 101** (18.9m) — **+14 new acceptance tests** and the same 3 pre-existing failures, no fourth. .NET **815/815** (Core **647 on three consecutive runs**, Guardrails 119, Integration 49). Render+visual clean at 1280 and 390, 0 console errors, 0 page overflow. `mockup-parity`: **`/effort` graded on its first run with a single finding** — the `app-sidebar clip@1280` that fires on all eleven screens (TF-012) — and the whole pass added **0 new findings** anywhere. **2026-09-02 VERIFIED (`*verify all`, Release build, booted rung #2 on :5099 against the configured `localhost:5550/tflens`).** Acceptance Playwright **95 passed / 3 skipped of 101** (the 4 reported failures are adjudicated below and in the run report; `00-boot` was a cold-start JIT timeout and passes on a warm app in 17.3s) + .NET **815/815** Release (Core 647 / Guardrails 119 / Integration 49). Render gate **487 controls over 22 screen-states, 0 RENDER-EMPTY / 0 RENDER-ERROR**; visual gate 1280+390, **0 overlap/clip/off-viewport, 0 console errors** (`tests/.artifacts/gates/render-visual.json`); asset-integrity **78 assets over 13 pages, 0 did not arrive, 0 redirects** (`tests/.artifacts/assets/run-auth.json`); mockup-parity **921 comparisons over 13 screens** (`tests/.artifacts/gates/mockup-parity.json`).
  - *Build 2026-09-11 [REQ-FN-103]:* **Root cause of the 2026-09-11 verify failure (presented 6, added 1, collapsed 5):** the Playbook key `UcMissUserRepoSourceLine` was partial, but the TechieFlow key `UcMissUserRepoMissId` was declared over **every** row — so a Playbook line repeating `miss_id` VRI-PB-1 with different content hit the TechieFlow key and was dropped by `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. The architecture already said the MissId key should govern TechieFlow rows only; the DDL never said so. **Fix (`database/001-schema.sql`):** `UcMissUserRepoMissId`, `UcMissFixUserRepoMissIdFixRunId` and `UcMissAmendUserRepoMissIdFieldTs` are now partial (`WHERE "SourceLineHash" IS NULL`), and `"MissFix"` / `"MissAmend"` gain their own Playbook keys (`UcMissFixUserRepoSourceLine`, `UcMissAmendUserRepoSourceLine`, `WHERE … IS NOT NULL`), so every row in the three tables has exactly one way to be unique. An existing database is migrated once: a `DO` block drops each old key only when `pg_indexes` shows it has no predicate (a lock-free check, so an already-migrated database pays nothing at startup or rebuild). Tests: `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Storage/PlaybookMissSourceLineKeyTests.cs` (live PostgreSQL — a later export adds a repeated-`miss_id` miss, a repeated fix run and a repeated amend field; TechieFlow misses still collapse on `miss_id`; the six keys exist with opposite predicates in the database and in the DDL). Smoked as test user 2 through `/repos` → Import: first export 4 added; same export again 0 added / 4 collapsed; later export presented 6, **added 2, collapsed 4**; `/misses` Playbook axis counts both CLA-ITEM-1 lines; the REQ-FN-103 verifier test passes. Not changed: the TechieFlow edition's behaviour, the store's insert statements, the normalizer.

<a id="d-req-fn-104"></a>
- **REQ-FN-104** — The two cross-edition axes are preserved as distinct columns, and the process/assertion gate pair never shares a column or a chart (BRD-165).
  - *Acceptance:* When cross-edition axes are read on `/misses`, then item id sits beside req id, and the process and assertion gates never share a column.
  - *Checks:* `ItemId` and `ReqId` are separate columns and a Playbook row populates the first while a TechieFlow row populates the second; `FoundPhaseGate` and `FoundGate` are separate columns, and a test asserts **no chart, table or export key** combines them into one series — the same guardrail shape that already keeps `phase_gate` apart from `gate` (BRD-74); a mixed dataset renders both axes without either being coerced into the other.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). One axis under two names vs two genuinely different measurements. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-104]` built: `"Miss"."ItemId"` and `"Miss"."FoundPhaseGate"` added as guarded nullable ALTERs, as their own `MissRecord` members and in `InsertMissSql` — never merged into `"ReqId"` or `"FoundGate"`. The distinction is documented where a later hand will read it: `ItemId`/`ReqId` is **one axis under two names** and may be read as one, while `FoundPhaseGate`/`FoundGate` is a **process** gate beside an **assertion** gate — two different measurements that never share a column or a chart (SCHEMA.md §11, ADR-010's rule applied to the shared table). Smoked against the real PostgreSQL: a Playbook row round-trips `ItemId`/`FoundPhaseGate` set with `ReqId`/`FoundGate` null, and a TechieFlow row the exact inverse. Not 100%: the chart-level separation on `/misses` and `/effort` is another cluster's half — this row supplies the columns. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-104]` **read half added** (cluster D): `PlaybookMissReport` exposes `ByItemId` and `ByFoundPhaseGate` and — by construction — has **no** `ByReqId` or `ByFoundGate` member, so a chart bound to the Playbook report cannot pool the two gate axes however it is captioned; a reflection test pins that shape. `ByFoundPhaseGate` reads `FoundPhaseGate` alone, proved by a miss carrying only `FoundGate` contributing nothing to it. Framework is a **required positional parameter** on every read path (`Read`, `ReadAsync`), checked rather than forwarded, with a reflection test asserting no default value — the architecture's stated residual risk is "a query that forgets the framework filter". **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-104] BUILT + VERIFIED — enforced by the type's shape, not by discipline.** `ItemId` sits beside `ReqId` and `FoundPhaseGate` beside `FoundGate` as their own nullable columns. `PlaybookMissReport` has **no `ByReqId` and no `ByFoundGate` member at all**, pinned by a reflection test, so a UI binding it *cannot* pool the two gate axes — the process gate and the assertion gate never share a column or a chart, the same rule and the same reason as `phase_gate` vs `gate` (BRD-74). The rendered surface carries them as two side-by-side columns. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`).
