<a id="d-req-fn-105"></a>
- **REQ-FN-105** — The Playbook's stricter miss reporting guards are applied as its own and are not unified with the TechieFlow guards (BRD-166).
  - *Acceptance:* When Playbook miss figures are read on `/misses`, then the stricter guards apply and an unknown origin never lands in a model-named bucket.
  - *Checks:* a Playbook attribution requires `origin_confidence:"linked"` **and** a complete valid source window **and** a non-null observed model — a record satisfying only the first is excluded and counted; a headline fix token or cost figure requires `cost_attribution:"sole"` **and** a complete valid window **and** `data_quality.cost_status:"complete"`, with `shared:<n>` shown separately as apportioned and `none` excluded; `sole`, `shared:<n>` and `none` never enter the same headline cohort; an inferred or unknown origin appears in **no** bucket named for a model — a test asserts no "unknown model" key exists in any per-model result or export.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). A bucket named for a model is a claim about a model. 2026-09-01 `[REQ-FN-105]` built (cluster D): `src/TfLens.Core/Playbook/PlaybookMissGuards.cs` enforces the Playbook edition's **own** guards — attribution needs `origin_confidence:"linked"` **and** a complete valid source window **and** a non-null observed model; the headline cost cohort needs `cost_attribution:"sole"` **and** a complete valid window **and** `data_quality.cost_status:"complete"`, with `shared:<n>` a separate apportioned cohort and `none` excluded as a count that is never a divisor. Refused records are reported by **reason** (`PlaybookGuardReasons`), never by model: `ByOriginModel` is computed over attributed records only and every attributed record carries a non-null model, so no `unknown` / `not-recorded` / em-dash row can appear beside real model names. Measured `cost_usd` never meets a rate-card `*_usd_estimate` — the estimate reaches no column at ingest and `PlaybookCostSplit` has no member that could hold the two together. `FIELD_SINCE` runs before the optional-field denominator through the existing `LateGateCoverageCalculator`, and the report publishes `n of N assessed`; every comparative figure below `MinN = 3` is `Figure.InsufficientData`. The class docs state in full why unifying downward with the TechieFlow guards is refused, and a test asserts the asymmetry on one shared record. **Spec gaps, sensible reading taken (both documented in the XML):** (a) the producer contract defers its field list to a `Telemetry-Guide.md` §7 TfLens does not hold, so the window is read from a nested `source_window {complete,valid}` and, as fallback, the sibling phase contract's spelling of top-level `complete` + `data_quality.valid` — **absent is never a pass**; (b) BRD-166 does not say whether an *apportioned* figure may rest on an invalid window, so the window test is applied to both cohorts and the `cost_status` test to the headline alone. **Not built here:** the Coverage / `/misses` bindings that render the diagnostics and refusals — engine-side only. **2026-09-01 [REQ-FN-105] BUILT + VERIFIED.** The Playbook's guards are enforced as **its own and are not relaxed to match the TechieFlow stream's** — unifying downward would weaken a claim the producer refuses to make, and the XML docs say so. Attribution needs `linked` + a complete valid window + a non-null observed model; a headline cost figure needs `sole` + a complete valid window + `cost_status:"complete"`, with `shared:<n>` shown separately as apportioned and `none` excluded. An inferred or unknown origin **never** enters an *"unknown model"* bucket — a bucket named for a model is a claim about a model. `Framework` is a **required, checked** read parameter (it throws on anything but `playbook`), and a live two-framework isolation test now sits beside the two-user one, which is the sibling the architecture asked for. **Spec gap:** BRD-166 fixes the window and cost-status conditions for the **headline** cohort and is silent on whether an apportioned figure may rest on an invalid window; the window test is applied to both, because the phase contract is explicit that a producer may retain zero-valued totals on an invalid row and apportioning one would publish a confident zero. `MISS-TfLens-20260901-12`. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). ## Non-functional

<a id="d-req-nfr-013"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-013** — Integrity, the miss invariants (BRD-130) — the NFR sibling of REQ-NFR-009 / BRD-89. **None of these has a configuration switch, a query parameter or a UI toggle that relaxes it:**
  - *Acceptance:* When the miss engine is inspected for a switch that relaxes an invariant, then none exists — the result types cannot express the forbidden figure.
  - *Checks:* (1) measured and apportioned rework cost are never blended into one figure — page, export or parity; (2) no per-model or per-agent figure is computed from `inferred` attributions, and the excluded count is never hidden; (3) the `WhyMissed` distribution is never rendered over all misses — the denominator is records carrying the field, printed on its face; (4) `wont-fix` is never folded into open misses, and the two open-predicates are never reconciled; (5) rate-card dollars are never presented as spend — `RateCard.EstimateLabel` on every such figure and `_usd_estimate` on every such key; (6) no miss record is ever folded into the `gates`-derived escape rate; (7) TfLens writes to no repository and emits into no stream, including `misses.jsonl`. Each clause is pinned by a test, not by prose.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-08-28 (F-MISS). 2026-08-28 [REQ-NFR-013] All seven clauses pinned by tests, none by prose: `tests/TfLens.Core.Tests/Metrics/MissInvariantTests.cs` (clauses 1-6 behavioural + the no-switch shape) and `tests/TfLens.Guardrails.Tests/MissInvariantContractTests.cs` (clause 7 + the result-type shapes). Clause 7 is proved as a negative over the tree: nothing in `src` starts a process, so no code path can run `tf-emit.sh` or `*log-miss`; no member anywhere takes a miss record as something to emit; and the writer list is asserted exhaustively so a sixth file writing stream bytes fails the build. Clause 6 is proved by running the engine twice over the same gates with and without misses and asserting `SegmentFigures.EscapeRate` is identical. 2026-08-28 VERIFIED (*verify all, chained inline from *build-phase): app booted Release on :5099 (rung #2); acceptance **77/77 Playwright** + **630/630 .NET** (`-m:1`); render gate — **450 controls across 22 screen-states, 0 RENDER-EMPTY / 0 RENDER-ERROR**; visual gate — 1280x800 and 390x844, **0 overlap/clip/off-viewport, 0 console errors** (tests/.artifacts/gates/render-visual.json); BRD §13 parity **exit 0, 0 findings** over 29 miss figures. 2026-08-29 re-verified (*verify all, Release build): Playwright 79 passed / 0 failed / 3 skipped of 82 (16.4m, exit 0); .NET 638/638; render+visual gate clean across 22 screen-states / 476 controls (0 render-empty, 0 visual problems at 1280 and 390, 0 console errors). **2026-09-02 VERIFIED (`*verify all`, Release build, booted rung #2 on :5099 against the configured `localhost:5550/tflens`).** Acceptance Playwright **95 passed / 3 skipped of 101** (the 4 reported failures are adjudicated below and in the run report; `00-boot` was a cold-start JIT timeout and passes on a warm app in 17.3s) + .NET **815/815** Release (Core 647 / Guardrails 119 / Integration 49). <a id="d-req-nfr-014"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-014** — The upload surface is bounded **in code**, and it is the only inbound path (BRD-139).
  - *Acceptance:* When the upload path is inspected from `/repos`, then it is the only inbound route and its limits are enforced in code, not by convention.
  - *Checks:* extension allow-list (`.zip`, `.jsonl`, `.ndjson`) and a 25 MB cap enforced **before** the body is read; zip extraction refuses absolute paths, `..` segments and symlinks, and caps entry count and total uncompressed size (archive-bomb safe); every written path is asserted to resolve inside `data/raw/<userId>/`; nothing uploaded is executed, and no uploaded content is ever rendered as HTML; the endpoint requires authentication and is scoped to the signed-in user; **no unauthenticated endpoint and no machine-to-machine ingest API exists** — a test enumerates the routes and asserts it. Each clause is pinned by a test, not by prose.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-08-28 r2 (F-IMPORT); 2026-08-28 `[REQ-NFR-014]` every clause pinned by its own test in `UploadBoundsTests`, `SafeZipReaderTests`, `TelemetryImportServiceTests` and `ImportSurfaceTests` (23 tests): allow-list `.zip`/`.jsonl`/`.ndjson`; 25 MB cap on the **declared** length, proven by an upload whose body throws if read; zip extraction refusing absolute paths, `..`, symlinks (Unix mode `S_IFLNK`), >512 entries and >100 MB expansion, with a real archive bomb refused on its expansion; `UploadBounds.TryConfine` proving every written path resolves inside `data/raw/<userId>/`; guardrails asserting nothing in the import area executes anything or renders raw HTML, that both routes are `RequireAuthorization()` and take no user id, and that **every** mapped route in the head is either authorized or on the closed `AnonymousRoutes` list. <a id="d-req-nfr-022"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-022** — No TfLens surface — page, API, export or parity — groups any quality, miss, rework, effort, token, time or cost figure by `actor` (BRD-168).
  - *Acceptance:* When any page, API, export or parity surface is inspected, then none can group any figure by actor, and no parameter could produce one.
  - *Checks:* (1) a guardrail test reflects over the metrics namespace, `IMetricsEngine`, `IExtraMetrics`, the miss and phase engines and every result type, and asserts **no method takes an actor parameter and no result key is keyed by actor**, under any spelling; (2) a grep of `src/TfLens/Components/**` and `src/TfLens.Core/Metrics/**` finds no actor grouping, filter, chart series or column; (3) a fixture Playbook dataset whose records carry distinct `actor` values produces figures **byte-identical** to the same dataset with the field stripped — the document-diff shape already used for `source_kind` (REQ-FN-087), which catches a caller that groups by it without naming it in a signature; (4) `actor` is retained on the stored row for provenance and appears in no rendered surface. Rationale: both AIFP contracts state the prohibition as a hard rule, and a dashboard that *can* rank people will be read as ranking people whatever its caption says.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). Both AIFP contracts state the prohibition as a hard rule; the Playbook stream carries the field for provenance, not comparison. Enforced structurally, like BRD-89 and BRD-130 — no parameter, filter or toggle can produce such a grouping. 2026-09-01 [REQ-NFR-022] **Implemented as a build-failing guardrail** — `tests/TfLens.Guardrails.Tests/ActorGroupingTests.cs`, 4 tests: no `.GroupBy`/`ToLookup`/`ToDictionary`/`OrderBy` keyed on `Actor` and no `…ByActor` / `ActorTotals` / `ActorLookup` member in `src/**/*.cs` + `*.razor`; no `GROUP BY` / `ORDER BY` / `PARTITION BY` on an actor column in `database/*.sql` (judged on the RAW line — every column here is double-quoted, so a literal-stripping scan would blank the column name); no `Actor` property on any aggregate result type (reflection over `TfLens.Core`); and a discriminator test proving the scan permits provenance (a stored column, a local read, an exported key, a rendered cell) and refuses only grouping/ranking/route/query. Escape hatch declared in the guarded source as `// REQ-NFR-022 provenance: why`. Every failure message names file:line, the matched text and the reason. Smoked by injecting real violations into `Coverage.razor` and `001-schema.sql` — both checks fired, both files restored. Suite **119/119**. **2026-09-01 [REQ-NFR-022] BUILT + VERIFIED.** Four build-failing checks over `src/` and `database/*.sql` refuse any actor-keyed **grouping, ranking or keying** — `GroupBy`, `GROUP BY` / `ORDER BY "Actor"`, a `ByActor` member, an `actor` route or query parameter, an `Actor` column on an aggregate result type. **Storing and reading `actor` for provenance stays permitted**, and a discriminator test asserts exactly that distinction, because a check that banned the column outright would be wrong and would be deleted the first time someone needed provenance. Failure messages carry the reasoning a reader who has never seen BRD-168 needs: the frameworks emit `actor` to say whose machine a record came from, not to compare people, and a dashboard that *can* rank people will be read as ranking them whatever its caption says. **SQL is scanned raw rather than literal-stripped** — PostgreSQL folds unquoted identifiers so every column here is double-quoted, and stripping literals was blanking the very statements to be caught; found in the author's own injection smoke and now pinned by two assertions. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). <a id="d-req-nfr-023"></a>
- **REQ-NFR-023** — The phase-effort and phase-efficiency invariants have no configuration switch, query parameter or UI toggle that relaxes them (BRD-169).
  - *Acceptance:* When the effort engine is inspected for a switch that relaxes an invariant, then none exists and no zero stands where a value was not measured.
  - *Checks:* (1) no rendered figure or export value is `0` where the underlying data is absent — a fixture with every optional §2.6 field missing produces text markers and `null`s throughout, and a test asserts no `?? 0` / `GetValueOrDefault()` / `or 0` on any of the five nullable effort fields; (2) measured and unobserved never pool, and every exclusion count is present in the rendered DOM beside its figure, not in a `title` attribute; (3) no effort tile, chart or export key carries a rate-card dollar figure; (4) **no per-REQ or per-feature effort view exists** — a test asserts no engine method accepts a REQ id on the effort path and no result type keys effort by `req_id`; (5) no per-subagent cost attribution exists; (6) the page carries no efficiency or ranking framing — the standing copy states it is a budgeting and capacity view and points quality at `/misses` and `/coverage`; (7) none of the above is reachable through a config key, query string or feature flag — the same structural guarantee as BRD-89 and BRD-130.
  - *History:* added by *amend-docs 2026-09-01 (F-EFFORT). The NFR sibling of BRD-89 / BRD-130 / BRD-168. `*build-phase` costing more than `*log-miss` is a fact about what those phases **are**. 2026-09-01 [REQ-NFR-023] **Implemented as a build-failing guardrail** — `tests/TfLens.Guardrails.Tests/PhaseEffortIntegrityTests.cs`, 8 tests, one per clause plus the no-switch clause and a discriminator: (1) no `?? 0` / `.GetValueOrDefault()` / SQL `COALESCE(…,0)` on the three §2.6 fields or on any of the ADR-026 denominators — TF-005, keyed to eleven names so ordinary `?? 0` on a real zero stays legal; (2) measured never added to unobserved, and `TokenWindow` / `FanoutObservation` must carry their counts when they exist; (3) no `EffortPerReq` / `DurationByReq` member, no REQ-keyed aggregation that also aggregates an effort concept, no per-REQ route or query on an effort surface — grouping gates by REQ stays legal; (4) no `RateCard` / `EstimateUsd` on an effort surface and no estimate summed with measured `cost_usd` (`/routing` and `/misses` keep their labelled estimates); (5) no per-subagent cost **attribution**; (6) no efficiency-scoreboard copy — the check reads whole sentences with markup and comments blanked, and passes a sentence carrying a negation cue, because BRD-169 *requires* the "not a ranking" disclaimer; (7) no `Allow…`/`…Enabled` switch, config key, JSON key or `TfLensOptions` property that relaxes any clause. Escape hatch declared in the guarded source as `// REQ-NFR-023 measured: why`. **Finding on existing code:** the first run reported `PlaybookAgentSplit.SubagentCostUsd` (+3 call sites) — a false positive: that is *measured* Playbook spend summed from the events' own `cost` field and `null` (never `0`) when absent, not an attribution, so the check was narrowed to the attribution verbs and to a subagent field meeting the rate card, and the reasoning is recorded in the test's XML docs. Smoked by injecting real violations into `Coverage.razor` and `001-schema.sql` — 7 of 8 fired (the effort-tile check correctly did not, `Coverage.razor` not being an effort surface); both files restored. Suite **119/119**. **2026-09-01 [REQ-NFR-023] BUILT + VERIFIED, and proven to fire.** Eight checks: no `?? 0` / `GetValueOrDefault` on the three §2.6 nullable fields or on a token-window or fan-out denominator (`TF-005` on a third stream); measured and unobserved never pooled; no estimated dollars on an effort surface; **no per-REQ or per-feature effort view** (a run's window divided across the REQs it touched is arithmetic dressed as measurement, and neither producer emits a per-REQ timing field); no per-subagent cost **attribution**; no efficiency-scoreboard framing in page copy; and no switch, parameter or toggle that relaxes any of it. **The checks were smoke-tested by injecting real violations** into a page and the schema — 8 of the 9 scanning checks fired, the effort-tile check correctly abstaining on a non-effort surface — and both files were restored byte-identical, verified by diff. **One first-run finding was a false positive and the check was narrowed rather than the code changed:** `PlaybookAgentSplit.SubagentCostUsd` is *measured* Playbook spend summed from the turn events' own `cost`, null and never `0` when absent; BRD-169 forbids **attribution** (dividing a run's cost or a rate card across subagents), not a producer-emitted measured total. The reasoning is recorded in the regex's XML docs so nobody re-broadens it. The copy check reads whole sentences and passes any carrying a negation cue, because BRD-169 **requires** the standing "this is not a quality scoreboard" sentence and a naive keyword ban would have forbidden the mandated copy. Routing is observed, never enforced. **Mockup now exists** — `the effort mockup`; the `⚠ no mockup` note above is superseded. 2026-09-01 [REQ-UI-049] **BUILT** (*build-phase, cluster E). `effort-routing` → `DataTable` `effort-routing-table` with routed / drifted / unknown, counts, `Progress` shares and a meaning line, plus a `By command phase` disclosure (`effort-routing-byphase`) carrying the same three counts per `cmd`. **`drifted` is styled INFO, never destructive** — TR-016 means `BadgeVariant` ships no Info member, so the tone rides on the shared `.tflens-badge-info` class from `app.css`. `unknown` keeps its own row rather than being folded into either side. Smoke: signed in as the documented demo user, rendered at 1280 and 390 — five KPI tiles across at 1280 and one per row at 390, 11 phase rows, no horizontal page overflow, no console errors; the period filter re-runs `PhaseMetrics.Compute` over the narrowed record set (105 runs → 58 on last-7-days). The *never destructive* clause is graded **three ways**, because any one of them alone is dodgeable: (a) the class list must not match `/destructive/i` and must carry `tflens-badge-info`; (b) the **computed colour** must not be the destructive colour — `--destructive` is authored in `oklch` and the badge computes to `rgb()`, so a raw string compare would pass for every colour in the product; both are put through `color-mix(in srgb, … 100%, transparent)` first and compared as sRGB triples within 0.06 (measured: destructive `1.006 0.391 0.404`, drifted `0.231 0.510 0.965`); (c) drift may not be *any* red — a red-dominance check (`r > g+0.15 && r > b+0.15`) rejects a different red as well as the token itself. The card footer is also asserted for the words. The `By command phase` disclosure is opened and required to carry all four columns and at least one row (today 11). **2026-09-01 [REQ-UI-049] BUILT + VERIFIED.** `routed` / `drifted` / `unknown` render, and **`drifted` is graded not-destructive three ways**: by class, by computed sRGB against `--destructive` (both normalized through `color-mix`, since `--destructive` is `oklch` and would never string-match an `rgb()` badge), and by a red-dominance check so a *different* red also fails. Routing is observed and never enforced; a red pill would assert a rule the framework does not have. Per-`cmd` counts sit behind a disclosure in the same card, the card face being the mockup's aggregated table. 2026-09-01 VERIFIED (*build-phase, verifier chained inline; ledger `docs/.last-verify.json`). <a id="d-req-fn-118"></a>
- **REQ-FN-118** — System shall fold `miss-amend` records into their parent **at read time, never at ingest** — oldest first, re-applying the null-check while folding (a merged stream can carry an amend and a later-written value in either order), applying only fields on the allowlist with values inside their closed vocabulary. An amend naming no known `miss`, or carrying a field off the allowlist, is an **orphan**: counted and surfaced on Coverage, never applied. `rebuild` shall re-derive identical values. *BRD:* BRD-116
  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-FN-075 (BRD-116); graded there.

There shall be **no second ingest code path** (BRD-132). Rows shall upsert on `(UserId, Repo, PhaseExecutionId)` — re-import is expected, because the exporter emits every currently readable window — and every normalized row shall retain `source_schema`, `source_harness`, importer version, repository identity and import timestamp. *BRD:* BRD-153
  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-FN-094 (BRD-153); graded there.

<a id="d-req-fn-129"></a>

  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-FN-095 (BRD-154); graded there.

<a id="d-req-fn-130"></a>
- **REQ-FN-130** — System shall keep **wall-clock elapsed**, **observed active time** and **human effort** as three separate concepts and shall never substitute one for another. Observed active time is the producer's **union** of assistant and tool intervals with overlapping and nested work counted once; `assistant_elapsed_ms` and `tool_elapsed_ms` are diagnostic sums that legitimately overlap and shall **never be added**. Observed active time shall never be labelled human effort, CPU time, utilization or additive compute; `coverage:"partial"` renders as an explicit **lower bound**, `unavailable` as no figure at all. Human effort is not captured and shall never be inferred from wall-clock or agent time. *BRD:* BRD-156
  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-FN-097 (BRD-156); graded there.

  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-UI-050 (BRD-162); graded there.

The `PlaybookEmpty` state (BRD-126) is what shows when no Playbook miss records have been imported — no longer the permanent condition of that axis. *BRD:* BRD-167

The raw value means nothing to a reader who has not read SCHEMA.md, and no reader of this page should have to. *BRD:* BRD-171
  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-UI-052 (BRD-171); graded there.

<a id="d-req-ui-070"></a>

A miss identified only by a class and a date is unrecognisable a month later, and a page nobody can read is not a page anybody checks. *BRD:* BRD-173
  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-UI-053 (BRD-173); graded there.

<a id="d-req-fn-131"></a>
- **REQ-FN-131** — System shall publish **`duration_measured_n`**, the number of live run records that produced a usable duration, beside every duration total. It is the total's **denominator**, and a total published without it invites the reader to take it for a figure over every run. *BRD:* BRD-189
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then its `phases` block carries `duration_measured_n` beside the duration totals, and it is never larger than the live run count.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-132"></a>
- **REQ-FN-132** — System shall publish **`duration_impossible_n`**, the number of records whose **`ended` precedes their `started`** and which therefore carry no duration and are excluded from every duration figure. TfLens shall **never substitute, clamp or guess** a duration for such a record, and shall **never edit or delete it** — the streams are append-only (SCHEMA.md §3); the record stays, the exclusion is published. *BRD:* BRD-190
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then its `phases` block carries `duration_impossible_n`, and a run whose `ended` precedes its `started` adds nothing to any duration total.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-133"></a>
- **REQ-FN-133** — System shall publish **`duration_absent_n`**, the number of records that **record no elapsed time** — a run that starts and ends in the same second, or one whose timestamps cannot be read and which carries no positive stored duration. A record that records no elapsed time shall be **reported in those words and never as corrupt, impossible or failing**. *BRD:* BRD-191
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then its `phases` block carries `duration_absent_n`, and neither the snapshot nor `/effort` calls those runs corrupt, impossible or failing.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-134"></a>
- **REQ-FN-134** — System shall publish **`duration_recomputed_n`**, the number of records whose timestamps **overrode a stored `duration_s` that disagreed with them by more than one second**. The tolerance is one second so that ordinary rounding is never reported as a disagreement. This count is what makes the override auditable: a total that moved because stored figures were overridden is a **different claim** from one read straight off the stream, and without the count the two are indistinguishable. *BRD:* BRD-192
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then its `phases` block carries `duration_recomputed_n` beside the duration totals.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-135"></a>
- **REQ-FN-135** — *(amended 2026-09-10 — see the amendment below; the rule below is the one in force)* System shall take a run's `duration_s` **from the record's own `started` and `ended` whenever both of them parse**, shall **override a stored `duration_s` that disagrees with those timestamps by more than one second**, shall treat a record whose **`ended` precedes its `started` as carrying no duration at all** and exclude it from every duration figure, shall count a run that **starts and ends in the same second as recording no elapsed time** rather than as corrupt, and shall fall back to a **positive stored `duration_s` only where the timestamps cannot be read**, treating `ts` as `ended` where `ended` is absent (SCHEMA's own definition). It shall state **how many durations were derived** beside every time figure built on them. A summed `duration_s` that skips records carrying two clocks and no duration **counts them as zero time while still counting their tokens** — so a phase's time covers one set of runs and its tokens another. The framework's reset reported **16h49m** under that bug against a true **55h57m**. The derivation is a **read-time** operation: TfLens writes to no stream (§3) and nothing is backfilled. *BRD:* BRD-179
  - *Acceptance:* N/A — duplicate of REQ-FN-112 (BRD-179); graded there.

<a id="d-req-fn-136"></a>
- **REQ-FN-136** — System shall parse and store **`billing_mode`** on a `runs` and a `miss-fix` record (SCHEMA §2.5b) — `subscription`, `metered`, `plan`, `local`, `mixed` or `unknown` — and shall treat a record written before 2026-09-10 as **`unrecorded`**, counted separately and never assumed into one of the five. A change of schema shall not silently rewrite the past. *BRD:* BRD-193
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then runs are counted by `billing_mode`, and records before 2026-09-10 count as `unrecorded`, never as a mode.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-137"></a>
- **REQ-FN-137** — System shall publish, per phase, **`money_usd`** over `metered` records only, **`plan_allowance_usd`** and `plan_allowance_by_model` over `plan` records, and **`money_mixed_usd`** on its own line for a window that ran models paid for in different ways — real money that cannot be separated from the part a subscription had already covered. Each shall carry its own record count. These three shall **never** be added together, and a subscription's zero shall never appear as spend. *BRD:* BRD-194
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then each phase carries `money_usd`, `plan_allowance_usd` and `money_mixed_usd` separately, each with its own record count, never summed.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-ui-071"></a>
- **REQ-UI-071** — System shall compute a **list price** for every run whose tokens were measured, at read time from a rate card, and shall publish it as **`list_usd`** with `list_usd_records` and **`list_usd_unpriced_n`** beside it. A model the card cannot price shall be **left out and counted, never priced at zero** — a phase that ran only unpriced models must not read as free. Per-model list price shall be attributed **only where the window ran one model**; splitting a mixed window's price by token share is arithmetic, not measurement. *BRD:* BRD-195
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then each phase carries `list_usd` beside `list_usd_records` and `list_usd_unpriced_n`, and an unpriced model is counted, never priced at zero.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-138"></a>
- **REQ-FN-138** — System shall publish the **owner-review** figures (SCHEMA §5.5.9) — `reviews_n`, `review_corrections`, `reviews_by_phase`, and the mean tokens to produce the reviewed output and to apply the corrections, each with the count of records carrying it. A review record is the **only record in any stream that prices a specification defect**, and a report showing build and verify costs but not review costs tells its reader that documents are free. Review records shall reach **no miss figure**: a review is not a mistake. *BRD:* BRD-196
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then it carries `reviews_n`, `review_corrections` and `reviews_by_phase`, and no review record appears in any miss count.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-139"></a>
- **REQ-FN-139** — System shall publish **`cost_usd_excluded_not_money_n`** — repairs whose dollars were measured and are **not money** (a subscription's zero, a local model's absent bill) — beside the measured-dollars figure and never inside it; and **`cost_list_usd_per_miss`**, the money-shaped figure that works on every harness, because a Claude Code repair can have no measured dollars at all. *BRD:* BRD-197
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then its misses section carries `cost_usd_excluded_not_money_n` beside the measured-dollars figure, never inside it, and `cost_list_usd_per_miss`.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-140"></a>
- **REQ-FN-140** — System shall honour **`kind: "run-void"`** (SCHEMA §2.7): a correction record naming one run by `cmd` + `started` and saying why. The named run shall leave **every** figure — never clamped, halved or guessed at — the void record shall never itself be counted as a run, and the counts shall be published as **`runs_voided_n`**, the reasons as `runs_voided`, and a void naming no run on the stream as **`run_voids_orphaned_n`**. Neither record shall ever be edited or deleted. Until this shipped TfLens read **81 live runs on TechieFlow against the reference's 75** — three corrections counted as work, and the three wrong runs they corrected still inside every total. *BRD:* BRD-198
  - *Acceptance:* When a user writes a snapshot from `/export`, then it carries `runs_voided_n`, `runs_voided` and `run_voids_orphaned_n`, and neither a voided run nor a void record is counted as a live run.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-fn-141"></a>
- **REQ-FN-141** — System shall identify a run record by its **line in its source file**, not by `(ts, app, cmd)`. That key is not unique: two `log-miss` runs land in the same second, and so do two `run-void` records naming different runs — and collapsing the latter silently un-voids a run. The streams are append-only, so line *n* is always the same record: re-parsing a file shall remain the no-op BRD-28 requires, while **two byte-identical records shall both survive**. *BRD:* BRD-199
  - *Acceptance:* When a user imports a runs file holding two byte-identical lines on `/repos`, then both count as runs, and importing it again adds none.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.

<a id="d-req-ui-072"></a>
- **REQ-UI-072** — User can see, on a **Price providers** screen, every provider a rate comes from — starting with Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenCode Go and OpenRouter — with each model's published rate, the page a person can check it against, and **when it was last checked**. User can add a provider and its endpoint, add or change a rate, and remove a provider. A provider that publishes rates as data shall be **refreshed from its own endpoint**; one that does not shall be marked as typed from its published page, and the two shall be **visibly different claims**. A rate the endpoint declines to publish (OpenRouter's `-1`) shall be **skipped, never stored and never coerced to zero**. A refresh that fails shall leave the stored rates unchanged and say so. Where two providers price the same model the first shall win and the clash shall be **reported, never averaged** — the mean of two published rates is a number nobody publishes. *BRD:* BRD-200
  - *Acceptance:* When a user opens `/prices`, then each provider shows its rates, source page and last-checked date, with endpoint-refreshed and hand-typed providers labelled differently.
  - *Mockup:* [prices.html](mockups/prices.html) — drawn 2026-09-11, after the page was built.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.
  - *History:* 2026-09-12 build: rebuilt on TrBlazeUI 2.1.0-ci.10. Every library workaround is gone from `Prices.razor` / `Prices.razor.css` — TR-001, TR-002, TR-009, TR-025, TR-026, TR-029, TR-031, TR-032 — and TR-016's real `BadgeVariant.Info` restored for the endpoint claim. The figure columns align through `Align="DataTableColumnAlign.End"`, cell density through `Density="DataTableDensity.Compact"` with each column's own padding merged over it, and the layout through `md:` utilities; the scoped sheet is down from 200 lines to 113 and holds no workaround. Two measured tests were added to `ui-prices.spec.ts` pinning the header alignment, the cell padding and the select chevron at 1280 and 390, each behind a guard that fails if the scoped stylesheet did not load — an empty `TfLens.<hash>.styles.css` would otherwise read as "the workaround is gone".

<a id="d-req-fn-142"></a>
- **REQ-FN-142** — A superseded model shall **keep its rate**. A record naming an older model is history and still has to report what it cost; dropping the rate would move those runs into `list_usd_unpriced_n` and make a past phase read as cheaper than it was. *BRD:* BRD-201
  - *Acceptance:* When a user opens `/prices`, then a superseded model still carries its rate, and runs naming it are priced in `list_usd` rather than counted in `list_usd_unpriced_n`.
  - *History:* 2026-09-11 verify: acceptance line written from the BRD item, because the row was appended with a placeholder and cannot be graded without one.
